/* one-off: re-drive grading for Celia's finished PT1.S3 (admin caller). delete after use */
import fs from "node:fs";
import path from "node:path";
import { fileURLToPath } from "node:url";
import { supabase } from "@/integrations/supabase/client";
const REPO = path.resolve(path.dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url)), "..");
function readEnv(file: string): Record<string, string> {
  const out: Record<string, string> = {};
  if (!fs.existsSync(file)) return out;
  for (const line of fs.readFileSync(file, "utf8").split(/\r?\n/)) {
    const t = line.trim(); if (!t || t.startsWith("#")) continue;
    const i = t.indexOf("="); if (i < 0) continue;
    out[t.slice(0, i).trim()] = t.slice(i + 1).trim().replace(/^["']|["']$/g, "");
  }
  return out;
}
const env = { ...readEnv(path.resolve(REPO, "../../.env")), ...readEnv(path.join(REPO, ".env")) };
const assignmentId = "3afbbbde-07df-41cf-871f-5106624c2988";
async function main() {
  const { error: authErr } = await supabase.auth.signInWithPassword({ email: env.LSATPREP_ADMIN_EMAIL, password: [REDACTED] });
  if (authErr) throw authErr;
  const { count } = await supabase.from("activity_item_responses").select("*", { count: "exact", head: true }).eq("assignment_id", assignmentId);
  console.log("responses rows:", count);
  const t0 = Date.now();
  const { data, error } = await supabase.functions.invoke("grade-rrc-section", { body: { assignmentId } });
  console.log(`grader returned in ${Math.round((Date.now() - t0) / 1000)}s`);
  if (error) {
    console.log("error:", error.message);
    const ctx = (error as any).context;
    if (ctx && typeof ctx.text === "function") console.log("body:", (await ctx.text()).slice(0, 1500));
  }
  console.log("data:", JSON.stringify(data, null, 1)?.slice(0, 2500));
  const { data: g } = await supabase.from("activity_section_grades").select("attempt_no,graded_at,grader_version,stimuli_graded,carried_pct,section").eq("assignment_id", assignmentId);
  console.log("grade rows now:", (g ?? []).map((x: any) => ({ attempt_no: x.attempt_no, graded_at: x.graded_at, stimuli_graded: x.stimuli_graded, carried_pct: x.carried_pct, complete: x.section?.complete, ungraded_count: x.section?.ungraded_count, stimuli_in_section: x.section?.stimuli_in_section })));
}
main().catch((e) => { console.error(e); process.exit(1); });

# Teaching Voice

How LSAT U sounds, across every surface. This is the canonical voice doc: route all student-facing and brand copy through it. Built from a grill with Joshua (2026-06-13), then tested and enriched against the full corpus of his real correspondence (944 messages he authored, mid-2025 to mid-2026, from the Gmail takeout; 2026-07-18). Claims below marked with a quote are evidenced by that corpus.

It replaces the old two-register / 15-named-tools "preserve verbatim" profile (deleted 2026-06-10) and folds in the brand/marketing register the earlier draft was missing.

## Core voice (every register)

The constant under all three registers below. This is Joshua on the page.

- **Quiet authority.** State the value and let it land. Never oversell, never beg, never perform enthusiasm.
- **Specific, never generic.** Real scores, the actual skill, the student's own history. Praise is a specific observation, not "great job."
- **Honest about the work, gentle about the person.** Be blunt that the LSAT is hard ("expect this to be exhausting and very challenging"). Never be blunt about a student's shortcomings. Honesty points at the material; warmth points at the person. When someone slips, nudge gently, never accuse.
- **Light, grounded metaphor, understated in email.** In his real correspondence the recurring image is quiet: "partner with you on your LSAT journey," "getting set up," "foundational." The vivid images (runway, climb, march toward November, an easy door to walk through) belong to authored teaching and brand copy, not to email. Keep metaphor light and grounded; never float off into abstraction.
- **Balanced rhythm.** Short declaratives plus one slightly longer warm beat. Air between ideas. Each idea gets its own line. Real messages are short and single-purpose (median ~280 characters).
- **Plain professional diction.** No corporate jargon, no startup hype ("unlock," "crush," "level up," "game-changer" — "crush" appears once in 944 messages). Contractions are the norm, not the exception. Exclamation points are genuine, not performed: a warm message often carries one, occasionally more, especially in openers ("Sorry for such a long delay!"). He is not exclamation-averse.
- **Forward-looking.** End on the next step or what's ahead.
- **No em dashes, ever.** Use periods, colons before lists, the middot (·) as a separator, and numbered steps for procedures.

## Openings, closings, and signature phrases

The mechanical fingerprint of his email, from the real corpus. Match these when drafting as Joshua.

- **Greeting.** "Hi [Name]," is the default (used ~519x). "Hey [Name]," for established/returning students (~229x, warmer). "Hello [Name]," (~105x) and "Dear [Name]," (~18x, formal) for first contact or a more formal note.
- **Sign-off.** "Best," and "Best regards," are the workhorses (~360x / ~237x). "Thanks," when it's a favor or logistics (~146x). "Warmly," / "Warm regards," (~54x) for a genuinely warm note. "Talk soon," (~9x) for someone he'll see again soon.
- **Signature.** Almost always signed **"Joshua"** (844 of 944 messages). The name is near-universal; include it.
- **Signature phrases (his verbal fingerprint, use naturally, don't overuse):** "let me know" (by far the most common, ~390x), "looking forward," "I hope" / "hope all is well" / "hope you," "no worries at all," "happy to" / "more than happy to," "just wanted to make sure," "make sure," "no rush," "of course."
- **Punctuation he actually uses:** semicolons (correctly, ~41x); ellipses essentially never. (For em-dashes, the hard rule in Core voice governs — never.)

## The apology / accountability move

One of his most frequent and characteristic behaviors, and worth naming on its own. When he is late to reply, or missed or mishandled something, he opens by owning it plainly, often with a brief honest reason, then pivots straight to the fix. No over-apologizing, no excuses dressed up.

> I'm so sorry. I thought I had let you know ... I clearly didn't communicate that as well as I should have.

> I've been away from work for personal reasons, but I'm back now and getting caught up.

The pattern: own it in the first line, give the real reason in one clause, move to what happens next. Warm, candid, brief.

## Situational micro-templates

Verbatim-grounded reference lines for the situations that recur most. Adapt, never paste blindly.

- **Student reschedules / cancels:** low-pressure, no guilt.
  > No worries at all. It happens, so please don't worry about it. If you would like to reschedule, just let me know.
- **He missed / no-showed (approved make-good template):** full ownership, real reason, offer to make it right (see the apology move above). When the cause was an unexpected health issue, the approved shape is: sincerely apologize, state that a serious health scare unexpectedly took him out of commission, offer a **completely free, full LSAT session**, and hand them the complimentary booking link `https://cal.com/joshua-jtx5ud/complimentary-session`. Emphasis stays limited: bold the complimentary offer, italicize the line that one focused session can put points on the board. Draft in Gmail; never send without Joshua's explicit go-ahead.
- **Consultation reminder (day-of):**
  > Just checking in before our consultation today. Looking forward to speaking with you. I just wanted to make sure everything is still good.
- **Consultation follow-up:** warm, specific to their situation.
  > Really nice meeting you. I enjoyed our conversation ... I'm glad we got to talk through where you're at and what you're aiming for on test day.
- **Inbound prospect (hasn't booked):** state availability plainly, no overselling, hand them the next step.
  > Thanks for reaching out ... I'd be happy to talk more about what you're working on and see if we'd be a good fit. You can schedule a consultation here.
- **Student under real-life stress:** meet the person first.
  > Please don't apologize ... it sounds like you are dealing with an incredibly stressful and demanding situation.

## The three registers

Which voice governs which surface.

### 1. Coaching (earnest) -- inbox, recaps, in-drill feedback, email

Fully earnest. Warm, sincere, calm, patient. No jokes. The closest thing to being in the room with Joshua. Normalizes struggle, then moves to the work.

Reference line (target tone):

> Totally normal to trip on this one. Let's slow down: what is the author actually concluding? Once you see it, the trap answers lose their grip. You've got this.

- **Encouragement (student missed a hard one):** honest first, then forward. Name that it's genuinely hard, stay calm, move to the work. Not soft-reassuring, not cold.
  > Missing this is expected. It's one of the hardest moves on the test. Let's slow down and see where it slipped.

## 2026-09-08 — Celia's RRC grade is stuck; the grader fix is committed but not deployed

Celia finished PT1.S3 (66 min, 112 MB webm) at 01:12 UTC and messaged at 01:34 that it "says it's still being marked". No grade row. Cause, confirmed in the Lovable Cloud logs: `grade-rrc-section` downloads the whole recording into memory and dies with "Memory limit exceeded" before transcribing. The four prior grades were ~20-minute runs. The sweeper is unscheduled by design, and re-driving dies the same way.

Fix committed and pushed: `2b3a0d6` (Deepgram fetches a 15-minute signed URL; the function never holds the audio) and `eecfb85` (a `build` marker on the 202 body and an `X-Grader-Build` header, so a deploy can be proven from outside). **Neither deployed.** Cloud shows the function last updated ~21:15 UTC with 22 deployments; every "Pushed from GitHub" entry since `843341d` reads "Build unsuccessful"; `deploy_project` published but did not touch the function; probes after both still answered without the marker and still crashed. `npm run build` succeeds locally, so Lovable's failure is not a code build error. Unresolved: why Lovable's builds fail, and how to deploy an edge function without Lovable's agent.

Also: Lovable shows a "Payment issue detected, will revert to Free" banner, and an open plan "RRC: line-by-line marking against the answer key" awaiting Approve (Joshua's, from 02:45 UTC). Not touched.

Critique of the section briefing page done: `wiki/RRC-BRIEFING-CRITIQUE-2026-09-08.md` (27/40; two P1s: the one-take/25-minute facts are not on the page despite the code comment, and the calming notice is styled as a fifth step card).

Temp scripts left in `projects/lsatu/scripts/` (untracked, safe to delete): `_grade-celia.ts`, `_poll-grade.sh`, `_poll-build.sh`, plus `.impeccable/live/`.

## 2026-09-08 (later) — Celia's run: the grader did deploy; the real fault is a 37-minute clock offset

Correction to the entry above: Lovable deployed `grade-rrc-section` at 04:03 UTC ("Deployed latest grade-rrc-section"). With the signed-URL fix running, Celia's run transcribed in 16 s and was refused by the clock proof: 3,848 words, 1,637 inside a window, last word at 102.5 min against windows ending at 65.9 min, drift 2,201 s.

Diagnosis from the file itself (downloaded, 112 MB, one WebM header, decodes to 1:56:14): the first 37 minutes are room noise at about −50 dB, her 65-minute run sits from minute 37 to 102, then 14 quiet minutes. Shifting every window by 2,195 s puts 93% of loud seconds inside windows (37% unshifted) and lines her Recall phases up with the loud stretches (8.7 dB louder than working, versus 0.6 dB unshifted). No time stretch fits. So the recorder ran about 37 minutes before the app's window clock started. Why the client did that is not yet known; the row timestamps agree with the windows, so it is not a sleep gap mid-run.

Shipped `ebe0477`: the grader scans for the shift that puts the most words inside windows, applies it to every window when ≥80% then fit, and writes `clock.alignment` onto the grade. Build marker `2026-09-08c`.

Also shipped and verified live today: the briefing page critique fixes, the "counting up" rewording (repo and DB, 24 rows), the staggered entrance, the type scale, and a "Recording" mark in the runner rail.

Open: why the recorder started 37 minutes early on Celia's machine (look at `useSectionRecorder.start` and what happens when she leaves and returns to the same assignment); the sweeper is still unscheduled; Celia has not been replied to.

# Diagnosing Bugs

A discipline for hard bugs. Skip phases only when explicitly justified.

When exploring the codebase, read `CONTEXT.md` (if it exists) to get a clear mental model of the relevant modules, and check ADRs in the area you're touching.

## Phase 1 — Build a feedback loop

**This is the skill.** Everything else is mechanical. If you have a **tight** pass/fail signal for the bug — one that goes red on _this_ bug — you will find the cause; bisection, hypothesis-testing, and instrumentation all just consume it. If you don't have one, no amount of staring at code will save you.

Spend disproportionate effort here. **Be aggressive. Be creative. Refuse to give up.**

### Ways to construct one — try them in roughly this order

1. **Failing test** at whatever seam reaches the bug — unit, integration, e2e.
2. **Curl / HTTP script** against a running dev server.
3. **CLI invocation** with a fixture input, diffing stdout against a known-good snapshot.
4. **Headless browser script** (Playwright / Puppeteer) — drives the UI, asserts on DOM/console/network.
5. **Replay a captured trace.** Save a real network request / payload / event log to disk; replay it through the code path in isolation.
6. **Throwaway harness.** Spin up a minimal subset of the system (one service, mocked deps) that exercises the bug code path with a single function call.
7. **Property / fuzz loop.** If the bug is "sometimes wrong output", run 1000 random inputs and look for the failure mode.
8. **Bisection harness.** If the bug appeared between two known states (commit, dataset, version), automate "boot at state X, check, repeat" so you can `git bisect run` it.
9. **Differential loop.** Run the same input through old-version vs new-version (or two configs) and diff outputs.
10. **HITL bash script.** Last resort. If a human must click, drive _them_ with `scripts/hitl-loop.template.sh` so the loop is still structured. Captured output feeds back to you.

Build the right feedback loop, and the bug is 90% fixed.

### Tighten the loop

Treat the loop as a product. Once you have _a_ loop, **tighten** it:

- Can I make it faster? (Cache setup, skip unrelated init, narrow the test scope.)
- Can I make the signal sharper? (Assert on the specific symptom, not "didn't crash".)
- Can I make it more deterministic? (Pin time, seed RNG, isolate filesystem, freeze network.)

A 30-second flaky loop is barely better than no loop; a 2-second deterministic one is tight — a debugging superpower.

### Non-deterministic bugs

The goal is not a clean repro but a **higher reproduction rate**. Loop the trigger 100×, parallelise, add stress, narrow timing windows, inject sleeps. A 50%-flake bug is debuggable; 1% is not — keep raising the rate until it's debuggable.

### When you genuinely cannot build a loop

Stop and say so explicitly. List what you tried. Ask the user for: (a) access to whatever environment reproduces it, (b) a captured artifact (HAR file, log dump, core dump, screen recording with timestamps), or (c) permission to add temporary production instrumentation. Do **not** proceed to hypothesise without a loop.

### Completion criterion — a tight loop that goes red

Phase 1 is done when the loop is **tight** and **red-capable**: you can name **one command** — a script path, a test invocation, a curl — that you have **already run at least once** (paste the invocation and its output), and that is:

- [ ] **Red-capable** — it drives the actual bug code path and asserts the **user's exact symptom**, so it can go red on this bug and green once fixed. Not "runs without erroring" — it must be able to _catch this specific bug_.
- [ ] **Deterministic** — same verdict every run (flaky bugs: a pinned, high reproduction rate, per above).
- [ ] **Fast** — seconds, not minutes.
- [ ] **Agent-runnable** — you can run it unattended; a human in the loop only via `scripts/hitl-loop.template.sh`.

If you catch yourself reading code to build a theory before this command exists, **stop — jumping straight to a hypothesis is the exact failure this skill prevents.** No red-capable command, no Phase 2.

## Phase 2 — Reproduce + minimise

Run the loop. Watch it go red — the bug appears.

Confirm:

- [ ] The loop produces the failure mode the **user** described — not a different failure that happens to be nearby. Wrong bug = wrong fix.
- [ ] The failure is reproducible across multiple runs (or, for non-deterministic bugs, reproducible at a high enough rate to debug against).
- [ ] You have captured the exact symptom (error message, wrong output, slow timing) so later phases can verify the fix actually addresses it.

### Minimise

Once it's red, shrink the repro to the **smallest scenario that still goes red**. Cut inputs, callers, config, data, and steps **one at a time**, re-running the loop after each cut — keep only what's load-bearing for the failure.

Why bother: a minimal repro shrinks the hypothesis space in Phase 3 (fewer moving parts left to suspect) and becomes the clean regression test in Phase 5.

Done when **every remaining element is load-bearing** — removing any one of them makes the loop go green.

Do not proceed until you have reproduced **and** minimised.

## Phase 3 — Hypothesise

Generate **3–5 ranked hypotheses** before testing any of them. Single-hypothesis generation anchors on the first plausible idea.

Each hypothesis must be **falsifiable**: state the prediction it makes.

> Format: "If <X> is the cause, then <changing Y> will make the bug disappear / <changing Z> will make it worse."

If you cannot state the prediction, the hypothesis is a vibe — discard or sharpen it.

**Show the ranked list to the user before testing.** They often have domain knowledge that re-ranks instantly ("we just deployed a change to #3"), or know hypotheses they've already ruled out. Cheap checkpoint, big time saver. Don't block on it — proceed with your ranking if the user is AFK.

## Phase 4 — Instrument

Each probe must map to a specific prediction from Phase 3. **Change one variable at a time.**

Tool preference:

1. **Debugger / REPL inspection** if the env supports it. One breakpoint beats ten logs.
2. **Targeted logs** at the boundaries that distinguish hypotheses.
3. Never "log everything and grep".

**Tag every debug log** with a unique prefix, e.g. `[DEBUG-a4f2]`. Cleanup at the end becomes a single grep. Untagged logs survive; tagged logs die.

**Perf branch.** For performance regressions, logs are usually wrong. Instead: establish a baseline measurement (timing harness, `performance.now()`, profiler, query plan), then bisect. Measure first, fix second.

## Phase 5 — Fix + regression test

Write the regression test **before the fix** — but only if there is a **correct seam** for it.

A correct seam is one where the test exercises the **real bug pattern** as it occurs at the call site. If the only available seam is too shallow (single-caller test when the bug needs multiple callers, unit test that can't replicate the chain that triggered the bug), a regression test there gives false confidence.

**If no correct seam exists, that itself is the finding.** Note it. The codebase architecture is preventing the bug from being locked down. Flag this for the next phase.

If a correct seam exists:

1. Turn the minimised repro into a failing test at that seam.
2. Watch it fail.
3. Apply the fix.
4. Watch it pass.
5. Re-run the Phase 1 feedback loop against the original (un-minimised) scenario.

## Phase 6 — Cleanup + post-mortem

Required before declaring done:

- [ ] Original repro no longer reproduces (re-run the Phase 1 loop)
- [ ] Regression test passes (or absence of seam is documented)
- [ ] All `[DEBUG-...]` instrumentation removed (`grep` the prefix)
- [ ] Throwaway prototypes deleted (or moved to a clearly-marked debug location)
- [ ] The hypothesis that turned out correct is stated in the commit / PR message — so the next debugger learns

**Then ask: what would have prevented this bug?** If the answer involves architectural change (no good test seam, tangled callers, hidden coupling) hand off to the `/improve-codebase-architecture` skill with the specifics. Make the recommendation **after** the fix is in, not before — you have more information now than when you started.

=== useSectionRecorder ===
import { useCallback, useRef, useState } from "react";

/**
 * The section's recording: one unbroken file, plus an index of what is where.
 *
 * The drill's own rule, from `corpus/loophole-online/drills/translation-plus-clir-drill.md`:
 * press record at the very beginning and do not end the recording until the
 * whole drill is finished, never pausing or stopping between steps or stimuli.
 * So this records once, continuously, and stops once.
 *
 * That rule and "organise the recording per portion of each question" only look
 * like they conflict. They are reconciled by an index rather than by cutting:
 * every phase boundary the runner passes is stamped as an offset from the start
 * of the file, so any portion -- a single chunk's recall, one stimulus's Read,
 * the whole of stimulus 14 -- is a seek, not a separate recording. Nothing is
 * ever paused, and nothing has to be found by ear afterwards.
 *
 * What this does NOT do is transcribe. Transcription happens off the machine
 * the drill is running on, against the finished file, so a slow or failed model
 * call can never touch a twenty-minute unbroken take.
 */

/**
 * Which portion of which stimulus a stretch of the file belongs to.
 *
 * Stimulus-grained: `working` is her time with the text up, `recalling` is The
 * Recall. There is no chunk field, because the app does not track which chunk
 * she is on (`docs/adr/0013-she-chunks-freely-and-the-app-does-not-track-it.md`);
 * what she covered inside a stretch is worked out from the audio afterwards.
 */
export interface SegmentBody {
  /** The stimulus this belongs to, by its content slug. */
  slug: string;
  /** 1-based position of that stimulus in the section. */
  order: number;
  phase: "working" | "recalling";
}

/** One stretch of the file, in ms from the start of the recording. */
export interface RecordingSegment extends SegmentBody {
  startMs: number;
  endMs: number;
  /**
   * A segment is stored verbatim inside `activity_item_responses.rrc`, which is
   * a `jsonb` column typed as `Json`. The index signature is what lets this
   * interface satisfy that type without a cast at the insert, so the shape is
   * checked rather than asserted. It sits here and not on `SegmentBody` because
   * `Omit` over an index signature erases the named members with it.
   */
  [key: string]: string | number;
}

export interface FinishedRecording {
  blob: Blob;
  mimeType: string;
  durationMs: number;
  segments: RecordingSegment[];
}

/**
 * Pick a container the browser will actually produce. Safari has no webm/opus,
 * so asking for it there yields a recorder that starts and emits nothing, which
 * is the worst failure available: it looks like it worked.
 */
function pickMimeType(): string | undefined {
  const candidates = [
    "audio/webm;codecs=opus",
    "audio/webm",
    "audio/mp4",
    "audio/ogg;codecs=opus",
  ];
  const supported = (window as unknown as { MediaRecorder?: typeof MediaRecorder }).MediaRecorder;
  if (!supported?.isTypeSupported) return undefined;
  return candidates.find((t) => supported.isTypeSupported(t));
}

/**
 * What the microphone is asked for, and what it is asked NOT to do.
 *
 * `{ audio: true }` takes Chrome's defaults, which are tuned for a phone call:
 * automatic gain, noise suppression and echo cancellation, all on. Measured on
 * a live 21-minute run, all three were active. For a call that is right. For
 * this it is not, and in three separate ways.
 *
 * The take is twenty-odd minutes with long SILENT reads inside it, so automatic
 * gain spends those reads winding the input up and then pumps room noise into
 * the gap before she speaks again. Noise suppression is a speech/not-speech
 * judgement made by the browser, ahead of ours, and the quiet end of her
 * delivery -- a trailed-off clause, an aside, the half-swallowed word -- is
 * exactly what it is most likely to decide is not speech. And what comes off
 * this file is not listened to, it is transcribed and then graded Detail by
 * Detail: a word the browser removed is a Detail she is marked as having
 * dropped.
 *
 * So they are asked for explicitly, and off. Mono because the source is one
 * voice and a second channel is bytes against a bucket cap, not information.
 *
 * The constraints are not `exact`. A device that cannot honour one of them
 * should still record the section: a run with automatic gain is worse than a
 * run without it and far better than no run. Nothing here can fail the drill.
 */
const AUDIO_CONSTRAINTS: MediaTrackConstraints = {
  autoGainControl: false,
  noiseSuppression: false,
  echoCancellation: false,
  channelCount: 1,
};

/**
 * Open the microphone, preferring the untreated stream and accepting the
 * treated one.
 *
 * Older browsers reject an unknown constraint key outright rather than ignoring
 * it, so a single request would turn "cannot switch off noise suppression" into
 * "no microphone" -- trading a slightly worse recording for none at all. The
 * retry is what stops a preference from ever becoming a requirement.
 */
async function openMicrophone(): Promise<MediaStream> {
  // No microphone API at all -- an insecure origin, or a browser without it.
  // That is the refused-microphone case, not a constraint the device would not
  // take, and retrying it would only produce the same TypeError twice.
  if (!navigator.mediaDevices?.getUserMedia) throw new Error("no microphone on this device");
  try {
    return await navigator.mediaDevices.getUserMedia({ audio: AUDIO_CONSTRAINTS });
  } catch (e) {
    // A refusal is not a constraint failure and must not be retried into a
    // second permission prompt.
    const name = (e as DOMException)?.name;
    if (name === "NotAllowedError" || name === "SecurityError") throw e;
    console.warn("[rrc] microphone would not take the drill's constraints; using its defaults", e);
    return await navigator.mediaDevices.getUserMedia({ audio: true });
  }
}

/**
 * How often the backup recorder hands over a piece.
 *
 * The most that can ever be lost to a crash. Ten seconds is short enough that
 * nothing meaningful goes missing and long enough that a twenty-minute section
 * costs about 130 small writes to her own disk, which is nothing.
 */
const BACKUP_SLICE_MS = 10_000;

export function useSectionRecorder(
  now: () => number = () => Date.now(),
  /**
   * Called with each piece of the parallel backup copy, in order.
   *
   * Optional, and the drill runs identically without it. The recorder does not
   * know or care where the pieces go; keeping the storage decision outside this
   * hook is what lets the backup be swapped or switched off without touching
   * the thing that produces the real recording.
   */
  onBackupChunk?: (blob: Blob, seq: number) => void,
) {
  const recorder = useRef<MediaRecorder | null>(null);
  const backup = useRef<MediaRecorder | null>(null);
  const backupSeq = useRef(0);
  const stream = useRef<MediaStream | null>(null);
  /** Kept in a ref so the callback can change without restarting a recorder. */
  const onChunk = useRef(onBackupChunk);
  onChunk.current = onBackupChunk;
  const parts = useRef<Blob[]>([]);
  const startedAt = useRef<number | null>(null);
  const segments = useRef<RecordingSegment[]>([]);
  const openAt = useRef<number>(0);
  const [error, setError] = useState<string | null>(null);
  const [recording, setRecording] = useState(false);

  /**
   * Ask for the microphone and start. Resolves either way, and reports the
   * refusal rather than throwing it.
   *
   * This used to say that a refused microphone must not stop the drill, because
   * the sitting is still valid against a handheld recorder. It is not: the
   * grader refuses a run with no recording, and no handheld file has any way to
   * reach the assignment. The runner now treats a reported refusal as a gate on
   * the ready beat (ADR 0029), so what this returns decides whether a section
   * begins at all.
   *
   * Safe to call again after a failure, and only after one: nothing has been
   * opened, so there is no recorder to restart.
   */
  const start = useCallback(async () => {
    if (recorder.current) return;
    try {
      const media = await openMicrophone();
      const mimeType = pickMimeType();
      const rec = new MediaRecorder(media, mimeType ? { mimeType } : undefined);
      parts.current = [];
      segments.current = [];
      rec.ondataavailable = (e) => {
        if (e.data.size > 0) parts.current.push(e.data);
      };
      rec.start();

      /*
       * The second recorder, on the same microphone.
       *
       * `rec` above is the real recording and is deliberately left alone: asked
       * for pieces as it goes, it can no longer write its own duration and the
       * file stops being seekable, which is what Clips are cut with. This one
       * exists only so a crash does not cost twenty minutes, and a recovered
       * copy only has to be good enough to grade.
       *
       * Its failure is swallowed whole. A browser that will not run two
       * recorders at once still runs the drill.
       */
      if (onChunk.current) {
        try {
          const copy = new MediaRecorder(media, mimeType ? { mimeType } : undefined);
          backupSeq.current = 0;
          copy.ondataavailable = (e) => {
            if (e.data.size > 0) onChunk.current?.(e.data, backupSeq.current++);
          };
          copy.start(BACKUP_SLICE_MS);
          backup.current = copy;
        } catch (e) {
          console.error("[rrc] no local backup copy for this run", e);
        }
      }

      stream.current = media;
      recorder.current = rec;
      startedAt.current = now();
      openAt.current = 0;
      setRecording(true);
      setError(null);
    } catch {
      // The name of the fault is not useful to her; the consequence is.
      setError("No microphone. The drill still runs, but nothing is being recorded.");
    }
  }, [now]);

  /**
   * Close the portion that just ended. Called on every phase boundary, from
   * inside the loop -- which is safe because it touches refs and no network.
   */
  const mark = useCallback(
    (segment: SegmentBody): RecordingSegment | null => {
      if (startedAt.current === null) return null;
      const at = now() - startedAt.current;
      const closed: RecordingSegment = { ...segment, startMs: openAt.current, endMs: at };
      segments.current.push(closed);
      openAt.current = at;
      // Handed back as well as kept, so a caller saving one stimulus as it
      // finishes can carry that stimulus's own portions with it. Without this
      // the index is only readable at `stop`, which is the one moment a crash
      // means it never arrives.
      return closed;
    },
    [now],
  );

  /** Stop once, at the end, and hand back the file with its index. */
  const stop = useCallback(async (): Promise<FinishedRecording | null> => {
    const rec = recorder.current;
    if (!rec || startedAt.current === null) return null;
    const durationMs = now() - startedAt.current;
    const mimeType = rec.mimeType || "audio/webm";
    const finished = new Promise<Blob>((resolve) => {
      rec.onstop = () => resolve(new Blob(parts.current, { type: mimeType }));
    });
    rec.stop();
    // Stopped before the tracks are, so its last piece is emitted rather than
    // cut off with the microphone.
    try {
      backup.current?.stop();
    } catch {
      // Already stopped, or never started. Neither is worth reporting.
    }
    backup.current = null;
    stream.current?.getTracks().forEach((t) => t.stop());
    recorder.current = null;
    stream.current = null;
    setRecording(false);
    const blob = await finished;
    return { blob, mimeType, durationMs, segments: segments.current.slice() };
  }, [now]);

  /**
   * The live input, for anything that needs to listen rather than store.
   *
   * A getter rather than state: the voice detector reads it inside an animation
   * frame loop, and re-rendering the whole runner when the microphone opens
   * would be a render in the middle of her first read.
   */
  const getStream = useCallback(() => stream.current, []);

  /**
   * The container the recorder settled on, readable from the first stimulus.
   *
   * The file name depends on it, and a row written before the section ends has
   * to name the file it will belong to. Falls back to the type the picker would
   * have chosen, so this answers even before the microphone opens.
   */
  const getMimeType = useCallback(
    () => recorder.current?.mimeType || pickMimeType() || "audio/webm",
    [],
  );

  return { start, mark, stop, error, recording, getStream, getMimeType };
}

/** Stable identity so hooks keyed on `now` do not restart every render. */
const defaultNow = () => Date.now();

/**
 * How long the ready beat runs before the first stimulus.
 *
 * Long enough to read one line and see the level move, short enough that it is
 * never in the way of a student on her fourth section of the week.
 */
const READY_SECONDS = 3;

/** A row gets a few quiet chances while she is still working, never an unbounded loop. */
const ROW_RETRY_ATTEMPTS = 3;
const ROW_RETRY_BASE_MS = 500;
const ROW_RETRY_MAX_MS = 4_000;

export function SectionRunner({
  assignmentId,
  studentId,
  items,
  sourceSection,
  timeGoalMinutes,
  accommodation = "standard",
  onComplete,
  onExit,
  now = defaultNow,
  readySeconds = READY_SECONDS,
}: SectionRunnerProps) {
  const run = useMemo(
    () => buildSectionRun(items, sourceSection, timeGoalMinutes),
    [items, sourceSection, timeGoalMinutes],
  );
  if (!run) {
    return (
      <div className="ac-section">
        <div className="ac-section-body">
          <p className="ac-section-instruction">This section could not be read</p>
          {onExit ? (
            <button type="button" className="ac-section-exit" onClick={onExit}>
              Leave
            </button>
          ) : null}
        </div>
      </div>
    );
  }
  return (
    <RunBody
      run={run}
      assignmentId={assignmentId}
      studentId={studentId}
      accommodation={accommodation}
      onComplete={onComplete}
      onExit={onExit}
      now={now}
      readySeconds={readySeconds}
    />
  );
}

=== recoverRecordings.ts ===
import { supabase } from "@/integrations/supabase/client";
import { requestSectionGrading } from "../api/activitiesClient";
import {
  assemble,
  discard,
  discardRow,
  isActive,
  isLive,
  listPendingRuns,
  listStashedRows,
  purgeStale,
  purgeStaleRows,
  type StashedRow,
} from "./recordingBackup";

/**
 * Upload the recordings of sections that never finished.
 *
 * A section that crashed, or whose lid closed at minute nineteen, left its
 * stimulus rows in the database and its audio nowhere. The pieces of that audio
 * are still on the student's own machine, and this is what puts them where they
 * belong the next time she opens the app.
 *
 * The rows written during that run already name the file, so uploading it to
 * that path is the whole of the repair: nothing has to be rewritten, and a
 * Report Card can then be produced from a sitting that was previously lost.
 *
 * Everything here is best-effort and silent. It runs on the way into a screen
 * she asked for, and a failed recovery must never be the reason that screen does
 * not open.
 */
export async function recoverPendingRecordings(studentId: string): Promise<number> {
  let recovered = 0;
  try {
    // Anything abandoned long enough that nobody is coming back for it.
    await purgeStale();

    for (const run of await listPendingRuns()) {
      // Not hers, and not one still being recorded -- in this tab, or in any
      // other. `isActive` is this tab's memory; `isLive` is the record's own
      // heartbeat, and the only one of the two a second tab can see.
      if (run.studentId !== studentId) continue;
      if (isActive(run.runId)) continue;
      if (isLive(run)) continue;

      const blob = await assemble(run);
      if (!blob || blob.size === 0) {
        // Announced and never written to. Nothing to recover and no reason to
        // keep looking at it.
        await discard(run.runId);
        continue;
      }

      const { error } = await supabase.storage
        .from(run.bucket)
        .upload(run.path, blob, { contentType: run.mimeType, upsert: true });
      if (error) {
        // Keep it. The next open tries again, and until then this copy is the
        // only one that exists.
        console.error("[rrc] could not upload a recovered recording", error);
        continue;
      }

      await discard(run.runId);
      recovered += 1;
    }
  } catch (e) {
    console.error("[rrc] recovery pass failed", e);
  }
  return recovered;
}

/**
 * Send the answers of sections whose rows never reached the database.
 *
 * The other half of `recoverPendingRecordings`, and the half that had nothing.
 * A run whose response rows do not cover its section is refused by the grader
 * and by the sweeper alike (ADR 0027), so a row lost to the network takes the
 * whole sitting with it -- and the recording, which very often uploaded
 * perfectly, could then be marked by nothing, ever. W2 stopped that being
 * silent. This is the way back.
 *
 * Runs after the recordings, because the grade needs the audio to be in place
 * before it is asked for.
 */
export async function recoverPendingRows(studentId: string): Promise<number> {
  let recovered = 0;
  try {
    await purgeStaleRows();

    const byRun = new Map<string, StashedRow[]>();
    for (const row of await listStashedRows()) {
      if (row.studentId !== studentId) continue;
      // A run this tab is still recording is not a run to be repairing. Rows
      // are only ever stashed at the end of one, so this is belt and braces.
      if (isActive(row.runId)) continue;
      const at = byRun.get(row.runId);
      if (at) at.push(row);
      else byRun.set(row.runId, [row]);
    }

    for (const [, rows] of byRun) {
      let landed = 0;
      for (const stashed of rows) {
        /*
         * One row per statement, and idempotent, exactly as the run's own
         * backstop writes it. `ignoreDuplicates` means a row that did in fact
         * arrive -- a response that succeeded after the tab gave up on it --
         * is a success here rather than a conflict.
         */
        const { error } = await supabase
          .from("activity_item_responses")
          .upsert([stashed.row as never], {
            onConflict: "assignment_id,student_id,item_order,attempt_no",
            ignoreDuplicates: true,
          });
        if (error) {
          // Keep it. The next open tries again, and until then this copy is
          // the only one that exists.
          console.error("[rrc] could not send a recovered answer", error);
          continue;
        }
        await discardRow(stashed.key);
        landed += 1;
      }

      /*
       * Ask for the grade only once the run is whole again.
       *
       * ADR 0027 refuses a run whose rows do not cover its section, so asking
       * on a partial recovery buys a certain 409 -- which is the thing W2
       * exists to stop happening in silence. `landed === rows.length` is the
       * last row of that run landing, and nothing else.
       */
      if (landed > 0 && landed === rows.length) {
        recovered += landed;
        void requestSectionGrading(rows[0].assignmentId);
      }
    }
  } catch (e) {
    console.error("[rrc] answer recovery pass failed", e);
  }
  return recovered;
}

import { describe, expect, it, vi, beforeEach } from "vitest";
import { act, fireEvent, render, screen } from "@testing-library/react";
import pt105 from "../content/read-recall-colour-pt105-s2.json";
import pt134 from "../content/read-recall-colour-pt134-s3.json";
import { SectionRunner } from "./SectionRunner";
import { isRunLive } from "@/lib/liveRun";
import { stashRows } from "./recordingBackup";

/** The local stash a row that never landed goes into, so it has a way back. */
vi.mock("./recordingBackup", async (importOriginal) => {
  const real = await importOriginal<typeof import("./recordingBackup")>();
  return { ...real, stashRows: vi.fn(() => Promise.resolve()) };
});

// The section runner, driven the way she drives it: one key, pressed twice per
// stimulus, start to finish.
//
// Everything asserted here is a rule the design states negatively, and each one
// fails silently rather than loudly if it breaks: a stimulus that stays on
// screen through its Recall, a verdict shown mid-run, or a write inside the
// loop all look like a working screen. She runs twenty unbroken minutes with
// nothing checking on her, so these are asserted rather than trusted.

type Row = Record<string, unknown>;
/** Every write of a stimulus row: during the run, and the backstop at the end. */
const upsert = vi.fn((_rows: Row[]) => Promise.resolve({ error: null }));
/** The one read: which Attempt is this. */
const attemptRows = { data: [] as Row[], error: null as unknown };
const upload = vi.fn(() => Promise.resolve({ error: null }));
/** The assignment close: `.update(patch).eq("id", id)`. */
const update = vi.fn((_patch: Row) => Promise.resolve({ error: null }));
/** The fire-and-forget ask for a grade. */
const invoke = vi.fn((_fn: string, _opts: Row) => Promise.resolve({ data: null, error: null }));
vi.mock("@/integrations/supabase/client", () => ({
  supabase: {
    from: () => ({
      upsert: (rows: Row[]) => upsert(rows),
      update: (patch: Row) => ({ eq: () => update(patch) }),
      select: () => ({
        eq: () => ({
          eq: () => ({ order: () => ({ limit: () => Promise.resolve(attemptRows) }) }),
        }),
      }),
    }),
    storage: { from: () => ({ upload: () => upload() }) },
    functions: { invoke: (fn: string, opts: Row) => invoke(fn, opts) },
  },
}));

/**
 * A microphone that works, because a section without one no longer runs.
 *
 * jsdom has neither `navigator.mediaDevices` nor `MediaRecorder`, so every test
 * in this file used to take the recorder's refused-microphone path -- which was
 * the wrong default even before it stopped working. The drill is spoken end to
 * end and the grader refuses a run with no recording, so the ready beat now
 * holds rather than counting down into twenty-five minutes nothing can mark.
 * The beat holding without a microphone is asserted on its own, below.
 */
class FakeRecorder {
  static isTypeSupported = () => true;
  ondataavailable: ((e: { data: Blob }) => void) | null = null;
  onstop: (() => void) | null = null;
  state = "inactive";
  constructor(
    _stream: MediaStream,
    readonly options?: { mimeType?: string },
  ) {}
  get mimeType() {
    return this.options?.mimeType ?? "audio/webm";
  }
  start() {
    this.state = "recording";
  }
  stop() {
    this.state = "inactive";
    this.ondataavailable?.({ data: new Blob(["x"], { type: this.mimeType }) });
    this.onstop?.();
  }
}

/** Granted, unless a test says otherwise. */
function grantMicrophone() {
  const stream = { getTracks: () => [{ stop: () => {} }] } as unknown as MediaStream;
  vi.stubGlobal("MediaRecorder", FakeRecorder);
  Object.defineProperty(navigator, "mediaDevices", {
    configurable: true,
    value: { getUserMedia: vi.fn(() => Promise.resolve(stream)) },
  });
}

/** Refused, the way a browser refuses it. */
function refuseMicrophone() {
  vi.stubGlobal("MediaRecorder", FakeRecorder);
  const denied = Object.assign(new Error("denied"), { name: "NotAllowedError" });
  Object.defineProperty(navigator, "mediaDevices", {
    configurable: true,
    value: { getUserMedia: vi.fn(() => Promise.reject(denied)) },
  });
}

/** One stimulus, so a full run is two presses. */
const ONE = { ...pt105, items: [pt105.items[0]] };
/** Two, for the rules about what never comes back. */
const TWO = { ...pt105, items: pt105.items.slice(0, 2) };
/** The 26-stimulus section observed in the audit. */
const TWENTY_SIX = pt134;
const CHUNKS = ONE.items[0].chunks;

async function renderRun(overrides: Partial<Parameters<typeof SectionRunner>[0]> = {}) {

  await act(async () => {});
  return { ...view, onComplete };
}

const space = () => fireEvent.keyDown(document, { code: "Space" });
const finish = (count: number) => {
  for (let i = 0; i < count * 2; i += 1) space();
};

import { beforeEach, describe, expect, it, vi } from "vitest";

/**
 * Recovery, in a tab that is not the one recording.
 *
 * `isActive` protects the live run from the tab that is making it. It cannot
 * protect it from any OTHER tab, and a student who opens a second section page
 * mid-run has one. These run recovery as that second tab would: same browser,
 * same IndexedDB, none of this tab's memory.
 */

const upload = vi.fn(() => Promise.resolve({ error: null }));
vi.mock("@/integrations/supabase/client", () => ({

}));

// Imported after the mock, and with `isActive` neutered: a second tab has an

  const real = await importOriginal<typeof import("./recordingBackup")>();
  return { ...real, isActive: () => false };
});
const { recoverPendingRecordings } = await import("./recoverRecordings");

const RUN: BackupRun = {
  runId: "run-live",
  assignmentId: "a1",
  studentId: "s1",
  attempt: 2,
  path: "s1/a1-a2.webm",
  bucket: "drill-recordings",
  mimeType: "audio/webm",
  startedAt: Date.now(),
};

beforeEach(async () => {
  vi.restoreAllMocks();
  upload.mockClear();
  for (const run of await listPendingRuns()) await discard(run.runId);
});

describe("recovery from a second tab", () => {
  it("leaves a run another tab is still recording alone", async () => {
    await beginBackup(RUN);
    await putChunk(RUN.runId, 0, new Blob(["header"]));

    const recovered = await recoverPendingRecordings("s1");

    expect(recovered).toBe(0);
    expect(upload).not.toHaveBeenCalled();
    // And, above all, did not throw the live run's crash protection away.
    expect((await listPendingRuns()).map((r) => r.runId)).toEqual([RUN.runId]);
  });

  it("still uploads a run whose tab has gone quiet", async () => {
    await beginBackup({ ...RUN, runId: "run-dead" });
    await putChunk("run-dead", 0, new Blob(["header"]));
    // Silence for well past the grace: the tab crashed. Age the clock rather
    // than writing a stale marker back, because announcing a run is itself a
    // claim that it is live -- `beginBackup` refreshes the heartbeat by design.
    const now = Date.now();
    vi.spyOn(Date, "now").mockReturnValue(now + LIVE_GRACE_MS + 1000);

    const recovered = await recoverPendingRecordings("s1");

    expect(recovered).toBe(1);
    expect(upload).toHaveBeenCalledTimes(1);
    expect(await listPendingRuns()).toEqual([]);
  });
});

=== recordingBackup 170-260 ===

/**
 * Runs this tab is recording right now.
 *
 * Recovery uploads a pending run on the assumption that nobody is still making
 * it. The live run is pending too -- it is only finished at the end -- so
 * without this a student who reopens a section mid-sitting could have her own
 * half-recording uploaded over the top of itself. Cleared when the run ends.
 */
const active = new Set<string>();

/** True while this tab is still producing that run. */
export function isActive(runId: string): boolean {
  return active.has(runId);
}

/** Announce a run, so a recovered pile of pieces knows where it belongs. */
export async function beginBackup(run: BackupRun): Promise<void> {
  active.add(run.runId);
  if (!available()) return;
  try {
    await (await db()).put(RUNS, { ...run, liveUntil: Date.now() + LIVE_GRACE_MS });
  } catch (e) {
    console.error("[rrc] could not open the local recording backup", e);
  }
}

/** One piece of the recording, in the order it was produced. */
export async function putChunk(runId: string, seq: number, blob: Blob): Promise<void> {
  if (!available()) return;
  await serialised(runId, async () => {
    try {
      const chunk: Chunk = {
        key: `${runId}:${String(seq).padStart(6, "0")}`,
        runId,
        seq,
        bytes: await toArrayBuffer(blob),
      };
      const instance = await db();
      await instance.put(CHUNKS, chunk);
      // The heartbeat. A piece just landed, so whoever is recording this run
      // is still here; say so where every tab can read it.
      const run = (await instance.get(RUNS, runId)) as BackupRun | undefined;
      if (run) await instance.put(RUNS, { ...run, liveUntil: Date.now() + LIVE_GRACE_MS });
    } catch (e) {
      // A full disk or a private window. The run continues; the primary
      // recording is unaffected and is still the real one.
      console.error("[rrc] could not keep a local copy of a recording chunk", e);
    }
  });
}

/** Every run that was started and never finished. */
export async function listPendingRuns(): Promise<BackupRun[]> {
  if (!available()) return [];
  try {
    return (await (await db()).getAll(RUNS)) as BackupRun[];
  } catch {
    return [];
  }
}

/**
 * Put a run's pieces back together.
 *
 * Sorted by sequence, never by insertion order, because only the first piece
 * carries the header and a file that does not start with it is not a file.
 */
export async function assemble(run: BackupRun): Promise<Blob | null> {
  if (!available()) return null;
  try {
    const chunks = (await (await db()).getAllFromIndex(CHUNKS, "runId", run.runId)) as Chunk[];
    if (!chunks.length) return null;
    chunks.sort((a, b) => a.seq - b.seq);
    return new Blob(chunks.map((c) => c.bytes), { type: run.mimeType });
  } catch (e) {
    console.error("[rrc] could not reassemble a recovered recording", e);
    return null;
  }
}

/** Forget a run: it is safely uploaded, or it is not worth keeping. */
export async function discard(runId: string): Promise<void> {
  active.delete(runId);
  if (!available()) return;
  try {
    const instance = await db();
    const keys = await instance.getAllKeysFromIndex(CHUNKS, "runId", runId);
    const tx = instance.transaction([CHUNKS, RUNS], "readwrite");
    await Promise.all([
      ...keys.map((k) => tx.objectStore(CHUNKS).delete(k)),

Tool result:
}

interface Chunk {
  key: string;
  runId: string;
  seq: number;
  /**
   * The bytes, not the Blob.
   *
   * IndexedDB can store a Blob, and storing one is the obvious thing to do, but
   * what comes back is not guaranteed to still be a Blob: it survives by
   * structured clone, and support has been uneven across browsers and is absent
   * in some test environments. An ArrayBuffer clones the same way everywhere.
   */
  bytes: ArrayBuffer;
}

/** `Blob.arrayBuffer` where it exists, and the older road where it does not. */
function toArrayBuffer(blob: Blob): Promise<ArrayBuffer> {
  if (typeof blob.arrayBuffer === "function") return blob.arrayBuffer();
  return new Promise((resolve, reject) => {
    const reader = new FileReader();
    reader.onload = () => resolve(reader.result as ArrayBuffer);
    reader.onerror = () => reject(reader.error);
    reader.readAsArrayBuffer(blob);
  });
}

function available(): boolean {
  try {
    return typeof indexedDB !== "undefined" && indexedDB !== null;
  } catch {
    return false;
  }
}

let dbp: Promise<IDBPDatabase> | null = null;
function db(): Promise<IDBPDatabase> {
  if (!dbp) {
    dbp = openDB(DB_NAME, DB_VERSION, {
      upgrade(instance) {
        if (!instance.objectStoreNames.contains(RUNS)) {
          instance.createObjectStore(RUNS, { keyPath: "runId" });
        }
        if (!instance.objectStoreNames.contains(CHUNKS)) {
          const store = instance.createObjectStore(CHUNKS, { keyPath: "key" });
          store.createIndex("runId", "runId");
        }
        if (!instance.objectStoreNames.contains(ROWS)) {
          const store = instance.createObjectStore(ROWS, { keyPath: "key" });
          store.createIndex("runId", "runId");
        }
      },
    });
  }
  return dbp;
}

/**
 * Writes, one after another, per run.
 *
 * Pieces arrive from a recorder callback and are written asynchronously, so two
 * can be in flight at once. Left to race they can land out of order, which is
 * the documented way these files get corrupted: the header stops being first
 * and the recording becomes unreadable. The chain costs nothing and removes the
 * failure entirely.
 */
const writeChains = new Map<string, Promise<unknown>>();
function serialised<T>(runId: string, work: () => Promise<T>): Promise<T> {
  const previous = writeChains.get(runId) ?? Promise.resolve();
  const next = previous.then(work, work);
  writeChains.set(runId, next.catch(() => undefined));
  return next;
}

=== discard 252-280 ===
export async function discard(runId: string): Promise<void> {
  active.delete(runId);
  if (!available()) return;
  try {
    const instance = await db();
    const keys = await instance.getAllKeysFromIndex(CHUNKS, "runId", runId);
    const tx = instance.transaction([CHUNKS, RUNS], "readwrite");
    await Promise.all([
      ...keys.map((k) => tx.objectStore(CHUNKS).delete(k)),
      tx.objectStore(RUNS).delete(runId),
      tx.done,
    ]);
    writeChains.delete(runId);
  } catch (e) {
    console.error("[rrc] could not clear a local recording backup", e);
  }
}

/**
 * A response row that never reached the database, kept where the recording is.
 *
 * The other half of the same failure. A recording that does not upload is
 * stashed above and re-sent on the next entry; a row that does not insert had
 * nowhere to go at all. It lived in the component's memory, she left the
 * finishing screen, and the answers were gone -- while the recording, which may
 * have uploaded perfectly, could then be marked by nothing, ever.
 */
export interface StashedRow {
  key: string;

=== SectionReport 14-115 ===
 *
 * So this polls for the grade rather than waiting on a response. The ABSENCE of
 * a row is the pending signal (ADR 0019): there is no status column to trust,
 * and none she could set herself. If it never arrives, the honest thing to show
 * is that it is still being marked, not an error and certainly not a zero. The
 * sweeper picks up anything this tab missed.
 */

const POLL_MS = 4000;
/** After this, stop asking and tell her it is still being marked. */
const GIVE_UP_MS = 3 * 60 * 1000;
/**
 * How long to wait before asking for the grade a second time.
 *
 * The runner already asked once, fire and forget, and that ask can be lost: the
 * tab closed before it left, the network dropped, the function was cold. One
 * repeat after a decent wait recovers all three.
 *
 * The wait has to be longer than a grading actually takes. At 20 seconds the
 * repeat arrived while the first grading was still transcribing, nothing was on
 * record yet, and both ran to the end: two transcriptions, two sets of verdicts
 * on the same audio, and a card that showed the first while the database kept
 * the second. A real section grades in one to three minutes. The grader now
 * also refuses to start while another grading of the same Attempt is running,
 * so a repeat that does land early costs one request rather than a second
 * grading; this wait is what keeps that from being the ordinary path.
 */
const RETRY_ASK_MS = 120000;

interface SectionReportProps {
  assignmentId: string;
  onDone?: () => void;
}

export function SectionReport({ assignmentId, onDone }: SectionReportProps) {
  const [grade, setGrade] = useState<SectionGrade | null>(null);
  const [stillWaiting, setStillWaiting] = useState(false);
  const startedAt = useRef(Date.now());

  useEffect(() => {
    let cancelled = false;
    let timer: ReturnType<typeof setTimeout>;
    let askedAgain = false;

    const poll = async () => {
      try {
        const found = await getSectionGrade(assignmentId);
        if (cancelled) return;
        if (found) {
          setGrade(found);
          return;
        }
      } catch {
        // A failed read is not a failed run. Keep waiting; the row either
        // arrives or the give-up copy takes over.
      }
      if (cancelled) return;

      const waited = Date.now() - startedAt.current;
      if (!askedAgain && waited > RETRY_ASK_MS) {
        // Ask once more. Losing the first ask is the likeliest way a finished
        // run ends up with no Report Card, and it is the one failure this
        // screen is in a position to repair by itself.
        askedAgain = true;
        void requestSectionGrading(assignmentId);
      }
      if (waited > GIVE_UP_MS) {
        setStillWaiting(true);
        return;
      }
      timer = setTimeout(poll, POLL_MS);
    };

    void poll();
    return () => {
      cancelled = true;
      clearTimeout(timer);
    };
  }, [assignmentId]);

  if (grade) return <SectionReportCard grade={grade} onDone={onDone} />;

  return (
    <div className="mx-auto max-w-2xl">
      <div className="rounded-[28px] border border-border bg-[hsl(var(--ac-surface))] p-8 text-center shadow-[0_2px_24px_rgba(30,24,16,0.06)] md:p-12">
        <p className="text-xs font-medium uppercase tracking-[0.2em] text-muted-foreground">
          Section finished
        </p>
        <p className="ac-display mt-3 text-4xl text-foreground">
          {stillWaiting ? "Still being marked" : "Marking your run"}
        </p>
        <p className="mt-4 text-sm text-muted-foreground">
          {stillWaiting
            ? "This one is taking longer than usual. Your run is saved and your Report Card will be here when you come back to it."
            : "Your recording is being read back against every detail. This takes a minute."}
        </p>
        {onDone && (
          <div className="mt-8">
            <Button variant={stillWaiting ? "default" : "outline"} onClick={onDone}>
              Done
            </Button>
          </div>

Tool result:
=== ADR 0033 ===
# 0033 — A phone is warned and let through, so it must be able to advance

Date: 2026-09-04
Status: accepted

## Context

The drill advances on Space and on nothing else. That is deliberate and it is
the whole interaction: one key, no mouse in the loop, so her hands never leave
the argument.

A phone has no Space key.

The 2026-09-03 audit found what that costs. The briefing, the door and the
library all render correctly at 390x844, so nothing before the run said a
keyboard was required. She could open the drill, read stimulus 1, and then find
there was no way to reach stimulus 2 — by which point the recording was already
running and the take was already spoiled. The only control on the screen was
"Leave".

The order (W9) put two options to Joshua and refused to pick: support the
phone, or refuse it at the door. The recommendation was to refuse.

## Decision

**Neither. Warn her, and let her through.** Joshua's call, 2026-09-04, against
the recommendation.

That answer carries a requirement the order did not state, and it is the whole
of this ADR: **letting her through is only honest if she can actually run the
drill.** A warning followed by a screen she cannot advance is not a choice she
was given, it is a trap she was told about. So the decision is two things, and
neither works alone:

1. **The door says what the run needs.** A notice on `SectionLaunch` when the
   pointer is coarse: this is built for a laptop, it records for the whole
   section in one take so a call or a locked screen ends the run, the phone
   microphone hears her less well, and she can carry on if she wants to. It
   disables nothing. Start stays exactly where it was.
2. **The run gives her a way to advance.** An on-screen control in the stage
   footer calling the same `press()` the keyboard calls.

**`(pointer: coarse)`, not a width.** The question is "is there a keyboard",
not "is the screen small". A narrow desktop window has a keyboard and must keep
the pure one-key loop; a large tablet has none and needs the control. Width
answers neither, and `useIsMobile`'s 768px breakpoint would have got both wrong.

**Space stays authoritative everywhere,** including on the device that gets the
button. The control is an addition for a device that cannot reach the key, not
a second way of doing things.

**The control is not rendered where a keyboard exists.** This is the part that
protects the drill. "No mouse in the loop" is a rule about attention, and a
laptop run is untouched by this decision.

## Consequences

- It is drawn as chrome, not as a call to action: a large filled button in the
  footer of a drill that judges nothing would read as a verdict, and nothing in
  that footer may.

{"file_path": "C:\\Users\\Joshu\\dev\\ai-os\\projects\\lsatu\\docs\\adr\\0034-a-recorder-dies-with-its-component-and-recovery-never-overwrites.md", "content": "# 0034 — A recorder dies with its component, and recovery never overwrites\n\nDate: 2026-09-08\nStatus: accepted\n\n## Context\n\nOn 2026-09-07 a student opened PT1.S3 at 23:28 UTC, left it, and came back at\n00:06 to run it properly. She finished at 01:12 and her recording uploaded. At\n01:30 her Report Card still said \"still being marked\", and the grader, once it\ncould read the file at all, refused the run: 3,848 transcribed words, 1,637\ninside a window, every window sitting 37 minutes before its speech.\n\nThe file in the bucket was 116 minutes long. Her run was 66. The first 37\nminutes were room noise; her section sat from minute 37 to 102; then 14 more\nquiet minutes. Storage showed the object created at 01:12:34 and **updated at\n01:30:49**.\n\nTwo things had happened, each harmless on its own.\n\n1. **The first sitting's recorder was never stopped.** `useSectionRecorder`\n   closed the microphone in `stop`, which only the finish path called. Leaving a\n   section, by the exit button or any route change, unmounted the runner and\n   left both `MediaRecorder`s running on an open microphone for as long as the\n   tab lived. That orphan recorded the next two hours, through her real sitting\n   of the same section and past its end, and its backup copy kept landing in\n   IndexedDB every ten seconds, each piece refreshing the run's `liveUntil`\n   heartbeat.\n2. **Recovery overwrote whatever was at the path.** When she reloaded after\n   finishing, the orphan died, its heartbeat lapsed, and\n   `recoverPendingRecordings` took it for a crashed run. Both sittings were\n   Attempt 1, because the first wrote no rows, so both named the same path. The\n   upload was `upsert: true`, and the orphan's 116-minute file replaced the\n   66-minute recording the real run had stored eighteen minutes earlier.\n\nThe grader has since learned to find the window clock inside a file (`ebe0477`),\nwhich is how the run was eventually graded. That is a repair, not a fix: the\nrecording of record for that Attempt is still the wrong file, and nothing\nstopped the same two faults from meeting again.\n\n## Decision\n\n**A recorder does not outlive the component that started it.** The hook closes\nboth recorders and the microphone tracks in its own unmount cleanup. Nothing is\nhanded back: a section she left is thrown away by the drill's rule (ADR 0023),\nand the finish path has already been through `stop` before the runner unmounts,\nso there is nothing there to close twice.\n\n**A run she leaves has no backup.** The runner discards the backup store's\nrecord and pieces for its run id on any unmount that is not the finish path.\n`discard` now takes its place in the per-run write chain and marks the run so\nthat a recorder's final piece, which arrives as an event after `stop`, cannot\nfile itself and put the run record back.\n\n**Recovery never overwrites.** The recovered upload is `upsert: false`. A\nconflict means the path already holds a recording, which can only have been\nwritten by a sitting that finished, so the pending pieces are surplus and are\ndiscarded. Any other failure keeps them for the next open, as before. Pending\nruns are tried newest first, so where two crashed sittings name one path the\none she actually ran is the one that lands.\n\n## Consequences\n\n- The microphone indicator goes out when she leaves a section, which it did\n  not before. This is visible and correct.\n- A student who leaves mid-run and returns to the same assignment starts on a\n  clean backup store. Nothing from the abandoned sitting can reach the bucket.\n- Backups already sitting in students' browsers from sittings abandoned before\n  this change will still be tried on their next open. They upload only where\n  nothing is stored, at the path of an Attempt that never finished, and cost a\n  file nobody reads; where a real recording exists they meet a conflict and\n  are forgotten.\n- The finish path still uploads with `upsert: true`, deliberately: it is the\n  run's own file and must replace anything an earlier recovery left at its\n  name.\n- What is not fixed here: the orphan ran for two hours because nothing in the\n  app tells the student a microphone is open outside a section. A page-level\n  guard is a different decision.\n- Regression tests: `SectionRunner.leave.test.tsx` (the recorder and the\n  backup on unmount) and the \"never overwrites\" block in\n  `recoverRecordings.test.ts`.\n"}

[]).map((r) => r.id);\n  const { data: grades, error: gErr } = await supabase\n    .from(\"activity_section_grades\")\n    .select(\"assignment_id, attempt_no, graded_at, section\")\n    .in(\"assignment_id\", ids.length ? ids : [\"-\"]);\n  if (gErr) throw gErr;\n  const { data: rows, error: rErr } = await supabase\n    .from(\"activity_item_responses\")\n    .select(\"assignment_id, attempt_no\")\n    .in(\"assignment_id\", ids.length ? ids : [\"-\"]);\n  if (rErr) throw rErr;\n\n  const latestAttempt = new Map<string, number>();\n  for (const r of rows ?? []) {\n    latestAttempt.set(r.assignment_id, Math.max(latestAttempt.get(r.assignment_id) ?? 0, r.attempt_no ?? 1));\n  }\n  const gradeFor = new Map<string, { complete: unknown; graded_at: string }>();\n  for (const g of grades ?? []) {\n    gradeFor.set(`${g.assignment_id}:${g.attempt_no}`, {\n      complete: (g.section as Record<string, unknown> | null)?.complete,\n      graded_at: g.graded_at,\n    });\n  }\n\n  const studentIds = [...new Set((runs ?? []).map((r) => r.student_id))];\n  const { data: profiles } = await supabase\n    .from(\"profiles\")\n    .select(\"id, name, email\")\n    .in(\"id\", studentIds.length ? studentIds : [\"-\"]);\n  const who = new Map((profiles ?? []).map((p) => [p.id, p.name || p.email || p.id]));\n\n  const now = Date.now();\n  const stuck: { id: string; student: string; section: string; finished: string; reason: string; age: string }[] = [];\n  for (const r of runs ?? []) {\n    const attempt = latestAttempt.get(r.id) ?? 1;\n    const g = gradeFor.get(`${r.id}:${attempt}`);\n    const finishedAt = r.completed_at ?? r.started_at;\n    const ageMs = finishedAt ? now - new Date(finishedAt).getTime() : 0;\n    if (ageMs < SETTLE_MS) continue;\n    let reason: string | null = null;\n    if (!g) reason = latestAttempt.has(r.id) ? \"never graded\" : \"no rows at all\";\n    else if (g.complete === false) reason = \"graded partial\";\n    if (!reason) continue;\n    stuck.push({\n      id: r.id,\n      student: who.get(r.student_id) ?? r.student_id,\n      section: r.source_section ?? r.title ?? \"?\",\n      finished: finishedAt ? finishedAt.slice(0, 16).replace(\"T\", \" \") : \"?\",\n      reason,\n      age: `${Math.round(ageMs / 3_600_000)}h`,\n    });\n  }\n\n  if (!stuck.length) {\n    console.log(`Every finished section older than ${SETTLE_MS / 60_000} minutes has a complete Report Card (${runs?.length ?? 0} checked).`);\n    return;\n  }\n  console.log(`READ, RECALL, COLOUR — ${stuck.length} finished run(s) with no complete Report Card\\n`);\n  for (const s of stuck) {\n    console.log(`  ${s.finished}Z  ${s.age.padStart(5)}  ${s.student.padEnd(22)} ${s.section.padEnd(10)} ${s.reason.padEnd(14)} ${s.id}`);\n  }\n\n  if (!grade) {\n    console.log(\"\\nRe-ask the grader for each with:  npx vite-node scripts/rrc-ungraded.ts -- --grade\");\n    return;\n  }\n  for (const s of stuck) {\n    if (s.reason === \"no rows at all\") continue;\n    const t0 = Date.now();\n    const { data, error: fnErr } = await supabase.functions.invoke(\"grade-rrc-section\", {\n      body: { assignmentId: s.id },\n    });\n    let body = \"\";\n    const ctx = (fnErr as { context?: { text?: () => Promise<string> } } | null)?.context;\n    if (ctx && typeof ctx.text === \"function\") body = (await ctx.text()).slice(0, 300);\n    console.log(\n      `\\n  ${s.student} ${s.section}: ${Math.round((Date.now() - t0) / 1000)}s → ${\n        fnErr ? `${fnErr.message} ${body}` : JSON.stringify(data).slice(0, 300)\n      }`,\n    );\n  }\n}\n\nmain().catch((e) => {\n  console.error(e);\n  process.exit(1);\n});\n"}

async function main() {
  const { error: authErr } = await supabase.auth.signInWithPassword({
    email: env.LSATPREP_ADMIN_EMAIL,
    password: [REDACTED],
  });
  if (authErr) {
    console.error(`sign-in failed: ${authErr.message}`);
    process.exit(1);
  }

  const { data: users, error } = await supabase.functions.invoke("admin-manage-users", {
    method: "GET",
  });
  if (error) throw new Error(`admin-manage-users failed: ${error.message}`);

  const q = query!.trim().toLowerCase();
  const rows = (users ?? []) as { class_id: string; display_name: string | null; email: string | null }[];
  const exact = rows.filter((r) => r.email?.toLowerCase() === q || r.display_name?.toLowerCase() === q);
  const pool = exact.length
    ? exact
    : rows.filter((r) => r.display_name?.toLowerCase().includes(q) || r.email?.toLowerCase().includes(q));

  if (pool.length !== 1) {
    console.error(pool.length ? `"${query}" is ambiguous:` : `no student matches "${query}".`);
    for (const r of pool) console.error(`  - ${r.display_name ?? "(no name)"} <${r.email ?? "?"}>`);
    process.exit(1);
  }
  const s = pool[0];
  const id = s.class_id;

  console.log(`\n${s.display_name ?? "(no name)"}  <${s.email ?? "?"}>`);
  console.log(`last ${days} days\n${"─".repeat(60)}`);

## assistant

The audit is done. Short answer to "will there ever be any issues": no system with a microphone, a browser, and a network is issue-free, but the two faults that actually bit this week are found, fixed, tested, and pushed, and there is now a tool that surfaces any run that gets stuck.

**The 37-minute mystery, solved from the data.** Celia opened the section at 23:28:48, left it, and came back at 00:06:27. Nothing ever stopped the first sitting's recorder, so it kept the microphone open and kept writing its crash backup for two hours, through her real run and past its end. When she reloaded after finishing, that orphan finally went quiet, the recovery pass mistook it for a crashed run, and uploaded its 116-minute file over her good 66-minute recording. Storage shows the object created at 01:12:34 and overwritten at 01:30:49. Both sittings were Attempt 1 because the first wrote no rows, so both named the same path.

**Fixed, red then green, pushed as 9098226:**

- The recorder hook now closes both recorders and the mic tracks when its component unmounts. The mic light goes out when a student leaves.
- The runner discards the backup of any run that unmounts without finishing. The discard joins the per-run write chain so a recorder's final chunk cannot resurrect the run.
- Recovery uploads without overwrite, forgets a run when the path already holds a recording, and tries the newest sitting first.
- Four new tests in [SectionRunner.leave.test.tsx](projects/lsatu/src/features/activity-centre/recall/SectionRunner.leave.test.tsx) and [recoverRecordings.test.ts](projects/lsatu/src/features/activity-centre/recall/recoverRecordings.test.ts). ADR 0034 records the decision.
- Full Activity Centre suite passes, typecheck clean, one pre-existing lint warning.

**A second student was stuck and nobody knew.** Charles finished PT1.S3 at 21:03 UTC on 2026-09-07 and sat on "still being marked" for eight hours. The old grader died on his 64 MB file and nothing retried, because the sweeper is unscheduled by design and the card gives up after three minutes. I re-drove it: 71% coverage, 16 of 22 coloured, graded at 05:16 UTC. A draft heads-up for him is appended to [the Celia draft file](wiki/CELIA-RRC-REPLY-DRAFT-2026-09-08.md), not sent.

**New tool** at [scripts/rrc-ungraded.ts](projects/lsatu/scripts/rrc-ungraded.ts). It lists every finished section older than ten minutes with no complete Report Card, and with the grade flag re-asks the grader for each. Until the sweeper is scheduled, run it daily:

```bash
cd C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os\projects\lsatu && npx vite-node scripts/rrc-ungraded.ts
```

**What is still open, and why I did not change it**

- The sweeper stays unscheduled. That is your recorded decision in ADR 0021, so a lost or refused grade reaches nobody unless the script runs. Scheduling it is a one-line change if you want it.
- A run the grader refuses for good shows "still being marked" forever with no message to you. The script covers this for now.
- Celia's stored recording is still the orphan file. Her grade is sound because the grader aligned around it, but the clips on her card seek into the shifted file.
- Backups already sitting in students' browsers from sittings abandoned before this deploy will still be tried on their next open, but can no longer overwrite anything.
- The send-section-report function fails its Deno check on a missing email dependency. That is the multiple-choice pipeline, not this drill.
- Temp scripts still sit untracked in the repo, deletion was permission-blocked: `_grade-celia.ts`, `_rows_tmp.ts`, `_diag4.ts`, `_dl.ts`, `_poll-build.sh`, `_poll-grade.sh`, and `.impeccable/live/`.

The vault log has the full entry dated 2026-09-08 05:20 UTC.

async function judge(prompt: string): Promise<string> {
  // Prefer a direct Gemini key when one is configured. The gateway is the app's
  // single LLM chokepoint and stays the default, but it is metered by Lovable
  // credits, and a grader that stops working when those run out is a grader
  // that stops working in the middle of a student's section.
  const direct = Deno.env.get("GEMINI_API_KEY");
  if (direct) {
    const res = await fetch(
      "https://generativelanguage.googleapis.com/v1beta/models/gemini-2.5-flash:generateContent",
      {
        method: "POST",
        headers: { "x-goog-api-key": [REDACTED], "Content-Type": "application/json" },
        body: JSON.stringify({
          contents: [{ parts: [{ text: prompt }] }],
          generationConfig: { temperature: 0, responseMimeType: "application/json" },
        }),
      },
    );
    const body = await res.json();
    if (!res.ok) throw new Error(`Gemini ${res.status}: ${JSON.stringify(body).slice(0, 200)}`);
    return body?.candidates?.[0]?.content?.parts?.[0]?.text ?? "";
  }

  const gatewayKey = Deno.env.get("LOVABLE_API_KEY");
  if (!gatewayKey) throw new Error("no judge backend: set GEMINI_API_KEY or LOVABLE_API_KEY");
  const res = await fetch(`${Deno.env.get("SUPABASE_URL")}/functions/v1/gemini-proxy/chat/completions`, {
    method: "POST",
    headers: { Authorization: [REDACTED] ${gatewayKey}`, "Content-Type": "application/json" },
    body: JSON.stringify({
      model: "google/gemini-2.5-flash",
      temperature: 0,
      messages: [{ role: "user", content: prompt }],
    }),
  });
  const body = await res.json();
  if (!res.ok) throw new Error(`gateway ${res.status}: ${JSON.stringify(body).slice(0, 200)}`);
  return body?.choices?.[0]?.message?.content ?? "";
}

/**
 * How long a claim on an Attempt's grading is honoured with no release.
 *
 * A grading that dies mid-way never releases its claim. Longer than any grading
 * has taken (the slowest observed was 163 seconds) and no longer than the

                    * after the run from the silence in front of each spoken
                    * line, so it is approximate -- which is why it says what she
                    * carried and dropped rather than scoring the line. Coverage
                    * above is unchanged.
                    */}
                  {!!s.lines?.length && (
                    <div className="mt-4 border-t border-border pt-3">
                      <p className="text-xs font-medium uppercase tracking-[0.16em] text-muted-foreground">
                        Line by line
                      </p>
                      <ol className="mt-3 space-y-3">
                        {s.lines.map((line) => (
                          <li key={line.n} className="rounded-xl bg-[hsl(var(--ac-surface-muted,var(--muted)))] px-3 py-2">
                            <div className="flex items-baseline justify-between gap-3">
                              <p className="text-sm font-medium text-foreground">
                                {line.n}. {line.text}
                              </p>
                              <span className="flex shrink-0 items-baseline gap-3">
                                <span

                                      ? "text-[hsl(var(--ac-emerald-strong))]"
