                                        ? "text-[hsl(350_80%_45%)]"

                                  </button>
                                )}

                            </div>

=== rrcLines.ts ===
/**
 * Cutting one stimulus's speech into the LINES she was rephrasing.
 *
 * The drill is line by line: she reads one line silently, looks away, says that
 * line back in her own words, then returns to the text for the next one. The
 * app never asks her which line she is on (ADR 0013) and it never will -- a tap
 * per line is ninety taps a section and one mistimed tap desyncs the whole
 * recording. So the split is worked out AFTERWARDS, from two things the run
 * already leaves behind:
 *
 * - **The silence.** She is silent while reading, so every spoken line arrives
 *   as its own burst with a gap in front of it. Bursts are the candidate units,
 *   and nothing here assumes she talks continuously.
 * - **The order.** She works top to bottom, so bursts map to lines in order.
 *   That makes this an alignment, not a classification: a burst can only belong
 *   to the line it is nearest to in sequence, which is far more robust than
 *   asking which line a paraphrase "sounds like".
 *
 * Wording is the tiebreak, not the decision. A rephrase shares content words
 * with the line it came from, so overlap picks between the two lines an
 * ordering leaves ambiguous.
 *
 * Nothing in this file is a boundary she is GRADED against. Coverage stays per
 * stimulus (ADR 0013); this exists so she can be shown, and hear, which line
 * each part of her run belongs to.
 */

export interface TimedWord {
  word: string;
  /** Seconds from the start of the recording, as the transcriber reports them. */
  start: number;
  end: number;
}

export interface Utterance {
  text: string;
  startMs: number;
  endMs: number;
}

export interface LineSpan {
  /** 1-based line number within the stimulus, matching the authored chunk. */
  n: number;
  /** The line as written. */
  text: string;
  /** What she said for it, joined in order. Empty when she said nothing. */
  spoken: string;
  startMs?: number;
  endMs?: number;
}

/**
 * The gap that separates one spoken line from the next, in milliseconds.
 *
 * She has to look back at the screen and read the next line silently before she
 * speaks again, so the real gaps are seconds long. This is set below the
 * shortest of those and above the pauses inside a sentence; it is a default
 * rather than a constant because it is the one number here that wants tuning
 * against real recordings.
 */
export const LINE_GAP_MS = 1200;

/** Break a stretch of words into bursts of speech separated by silence. */
export function splitUtterances(words: TimedWord[], gapMs = LINE_GAP_MS): Utterance[] {
  const out: Utterance[] = [];
  let current: TimedWord[] = [];
  let previousEnd = 0;

  const flush = () => {
    if (!current.length) return;
    out.push({
      text: current.map((w) => w.word).join(" ").trim(),
      startMs: Math.round(current[0].start * 1000),
      endMs: Math.round(current[current.length - 1].end * 1000),
    });
    current = [];
  };

  for (const w of words) {
    if (current.length && w.start * 1000 - previousEnd >= gapMs) flush();
    current.push(w);
    previousEnd = w.end * 1000;
  }
  flush();
  return out;
}

const STOP = new Set(
  ("a an and are as at be been but by for from had has have he her his i if in is it its of on or " +
    "she so that the their then there they this to was were what which who will with you your not no " +
    "do does did been being them these those we us our").split(" "),
);

function contentTokens(text: string): string[] {
  return text
    .toLowerCase()
    .replace(/[^a-z0-9' ]+/g, " ")
    .split(/\s+/)
    .filter((t) => t.length > 2 && !STOP.has(t));
}

/**
 * How much a burst sounds like it came from a given line.
 *
 * Content words only, and symmetric-ish: a long burst about a short line should
 * not be punished for saying more than the line did. Zero is a legitimate
 * answer -- a pure reaction ("wait, that can't be right") shares no words with
 * anything -- which is why ordering, not this, decides the attribution.
 */
export function lineSimilarity(spoken: string, line: string): number {
  const a = new Set(contentTokens(spoken));
  const b = contentTokens(line);
  if (!a.size || !b.length) return 0;
  const hits = b.filter((t) => a.has(t)).length;
  return hits / b.length;
}

/**
 * Attribute bursts to lines, in order.
 *
 * A monotone alignment: burst 1 cannot belong to a later line than burst 2. The
 * best such alignment is found exactly rather than greedily, because a greedy
 * pass that mis-assigns one burst pushes every later burst onto the wrong line
 * and the whole stimulus reads as somebody else's work -- the same failure the
 * transcript slicing already had once.
 *
 * A line she never spoke about comes back with an empty `spoken`, which is a
 * skip and must never be read as a pass.
 */
export function attributeLines(utterances: Utterance[], lines: string[]): LineSpan[] {
  const spans: LineSpan[] = lines.map((text, i) => ({ n: i + 1, text, spoken: "" }));
  if (!lines.length || !utterances.length) return spans;

  const m = utterances.length;
  const k = lines.length;
  const sim: number[][] = utterances.map((u) => lines.map((l) => lineSimilarity(u.text, l)));

  // dp[i][j]: best score having placed the first i bursts within the first j
  // lines. Either line j takes nothing, or it takes burst i.
  const dp: number[][] = Array.from({ length: m + 1 }, () => new Array(k + 1).fill(-Infinity));
  for (let j = 0; j <= k; j++) dp[0][j] = 0;
  for (let i = 1; i <= m; i++) {
    for (let j = 1; j <= k; j++) {
      const skipLine = dp[i][j - 1];
      const takeBurst = dp[i - 1][j] === -Infinity ? -Infinity : dp[i - 1][j] + sim[i - 1][j - 1];
      dp[i][j] = Math.max(skipLine, takeBurst);
    }
  }

  const assigned = new Array<number>(m).fill(k - 1);
  let i = m;
  let j = k;
  while (i > 0 && j > 0) {
    const takeBurst = dp[i - 1][j] === -Infinity ? -Infinity : dp[i - 1][j] + sim[i - 1][j - 1];
    if (dp[i][j] === takeBurst) {
      assigned[i - 1] = j - 1;
      i--;
    } else {
      j--;
    }
  }
  // Anything left over belongs to the first line: she cannot have been ahead of
  // where she started.
  for (; i > 0; i--) assigned[i - 1] = 0;

  utterances.forEach((u, index) => {
    const line = spans[assigned[index]];
    line.spoken = line.spoken ? `${line.spoken} ${u.text}` : u.text;
    line.startMs = line.startMs === undefined ? u.startMs : Math.min(line.startMs, u.startMs);
    line.endMs = line.endMs === undefined ? u.endMs : Math.max(line.endMs, u.endMs);
  });

  return spans;
}

/** The whole job: the words she said with the text on screen, cut into lines. */
export function linesFromWords(
  workingWords: TimedWord[],
  lines: string[],
  gapMs = LINE_GAP_MS,
): LineSpan[] {
  return attributeLines(splitUtterances(workingWords, gapMs), lines);
}

from memory; then the next line, and so on. When every line is done the text
disappears and she restates the whole stimulus from memory. She never reads
aloud. Your job is to decide, for each numbered Detail, whether she carried it.

${input.stimulus}
"""

  readGiven: boolean;
  readMatches: boolean;

  /**
   * Line-by-line feedback, in authored order. Empty when the run could not be

   */

  recall?: { verdict: LineVerdict; note: string };

  bigMisses: string[];
}

export function gradeStimulus(
  entries: JudgeEntry[],
  reply: JudgeReply,
  /** Her run cut into lines, when it could be. Feedback only, never scored. */
  lineSpans: { n: number; text: string; spoken: string; startMs?: number; endMs?: number }[] = [],
): StimulusGrade {
  const own = entries.filter((e) => !e.decoy);
  const carriedEntries = own.filter((e) => reply.verdicts[e.id] === true);
  const total = own.length;
  // Guard the empty stimulus rather than dividing by zero into NaN, which
  // renders as a blank band and looks like a rendering bug.
  const percent = total === 0 ? 0 : Math.round((carriedEntries.length / total) * 100);

  const lines: LineGrade[] = lineSpans.map((span) => {
    const forLine = own.filter((e) => e.line === span.n);
    const judged = reply.lineVerdicts[span.n];
    return {
      n: span.n,
      text: span.text,
      spoken: span.spoken,
      startMs: span.startMs,
      endMs: span.endMs,
      // She said nothing here, so it is a skip whatever the judge called it.
      verdict: span.spoken.trim() === "" ? "skipped" : (judged?.verdict ?? "thin"),
      note: judged?.note ?? "",
      carried: forLine.filter((e) => reply.verdicts[e.id] === true).map((e) => e.text),
      dropped: forLine.filter((e) => reply.verdicts[e.id] !== true).map((e) => e.text),
    };
  });

  return {
    carried: carriedEntries.length,
    total,
    percent,
    band: bandFor(percent),
    missed: own.filter((e) => reply.verdicts[e.id] !== true).map((e) => e.text),
    decoysCarried: entries.filter((e) => e.decoy && reply.verdicts[e.id] === true).map((e) => e.text),
    unjudged: own.filter((e) => reply.missingIds.includes(e.id)).map((e) => e.text),
    reciting: reply.reciting,
    listing: reply.listing,
    coloured: reply.coloured,
    colourSpecific: reply.colourSpecific,
    readGiven: reply.readGiven,
    // A Read that was never given cannot have matched. Enforced here rather
    // than trusted, so a judge answering both true cannot invent one.
    readMatches: reply.readGiven && reply.readMatches,
    readReason: reply.readReason,
    lines,
    recall: reply.recallVerdict,
    bigMisses: reply.bigMisses,
  };

}

Walk back.\n  let i = n;\n  for (let j = k; j >= 1; j--) {\n    const p = from[j][i];\n    if (p === -1) continue;\n    const span = spans[j - 1];\n    const taken = words.slice(p, i);\n    span.spoken = taken.map((w) => w.word).join(\" \").trim();\n    span.startMs = Math.round(taken[0].start * 1000);\n    span.endMs = Math.round(taken[taken.length - 1].end * 1000);\n    i = p;\n  }\n  // The DP always reaches i = 0 (line 1 may take from word 0), so nothing is\n  // left over; if it ever were, it belongs to the first line.\n  if (i > 0) {\n    const first = spans[0];\n    const taken = words.slice(0, i);\n    first.spoken = `${taken.map((w) => w.word).join(\" \").trim()} ${first.spoken}`.trim();\n    first.startMs = Math.round(taken[0].start * 1000);\n    first.endMs = first.endMs ?? Straight answers to your questions, then what is left.

**Is it using the Gemini key you added?** Yes, verified live. I added an admin-only diagnose call to the grader, and at 05:35 UTC it answered: judge is gemini-direct on your key, model gemini-3.8-flash, one real call succeeded, transcriber is Deepgram. Your model change and key are what is running.

**Can she see her verbatim transcript, paired to each line?** Yes, and as of now it is paired correctly. The Report Card already showed the full transcript per stimulus, the golden line text, her words for that line, a verdict, the dropped details for that line, "Biggest misses", her Read verdict, and a "Hear it" clip per line. The pairing was broken though: it cut only at silences of over 1.2 seconds, so when Celia retold three lines in one breath everything landed on line 1 and lines 2 and 3 said "You did not say anything for this line" while their own detail lists said she carried them. I rebuilt the split to assign each line its own stretch of her words by content, in order, cutting at full stops and silences. On her real run, empty lines fell from most to 6 of 74, and the mid-phrase cut ("The mathe | theory") is gone. Six Deno tests, two built from her actual stimuli. Deployed and her card re-graded at 05:45 UTC.

**Is it perfectly functioning?** No, and here is the one thing that is your decision, not mine. Switching the judge to gemini-3.8-flash changed the answers:

| Celia, PT1.S3 | 2.5-flash (04:47) | 3.8-flash (05:45) |
|---|---|---|
| Mean coverage | 89 | 86 |
| Stimulus 1 | 82% | 59% |
| Coloured | 6 of 22 | 0 of 22 |
| Reads given | 1 | 0 |

On stimulus 1 she said "even antibiotics that are strong still struggle to keep bacteria out" and 3.8 now marks "even the strong ones fail" as dropped. The bands and the colour rule were calibrated against your model runs on 2.5-flash (ADRs 0017 and 0018), and that calibration does not carry over. Charles was graded on 2.5 and Celia on 3.8, so their cards are not comparable. Two options: re-run the golden-standard census on 3.8 and reset the bands, or pin the judge back to 2.5-flash. I did not pick for you.

## user

Base directory for this skill: C:\Users\Joshu\.claude\skills\session-handoff

# Session Handoff

Produce a repeatable end-of-session summary so Joshua can `/clear` and start a fresh agent without losing continuity. The next agent should pick up by reading this summary alone.

This is a **context-handoff artifact**, not a status report. The audience is a future instance of you, not a stakeholder.

**Handoff vs. wrapup:** run `session-handoff` when the *context window* is the problem — nearing ~250k tokens (the dumb zone), or passing work between Claude and Codex. It is chat-only and writes nothing. Run `wrapup` when the *work day* is done — it strikes tasks, refreshes `wiki/hot.md`, and appends `wiki/log.md`. They can be run back to back (wrapup to persist, then handoff to reset).

## When to invoke

Joshua says: "session handoff", "hand off", "handoff summary", "summarize before I clear", or any near-equivalent. Note "let's wrap up" belongs to `wrapup`, not here — if he says that and the context window is also deep, run `wrapup` first, then offer this. Also invoke proactively if he says he's about to `/clear` without having run it, or if the session is clearly deep into the dumb zone.

## How to produce the summary

1. **Review the full conversation**, not just the last few turns. Handoffs miss things when they only summarize recent context.
2. **Pull state from these sources (in order):**
   - Plan files referenced this session (check `~/.claude/plans/` if a plan was mentioned).
   - TodoWrite state — any in-progress or pending tasks.
   - Background processes started with `run_in_background` — shell IDs are load-bearing for the next agent.
   - Files created or modified this session — you know what you touched; don't grep to re-discover.
   - Memory files written or updated (`~/.claude/projects/C--Users-Joshu-dev-ai-os/memory/`).
   - Unresolved questions — things you asked Joshua that never got a clear answer, or things he asked that got deflected.
3. **Do NOT audit the filesystem.** This is synthesis of what happened in THIS session. No `git log`, no broad `Glob` sweeps. If you didn't touch it this session, it doesn't belong here.
4. **Produce the output in chat.** Do not write a file. Do not update memory. Chat-only.

## Output template — use exactly this structure, every time

```
# Session Handoff — <one-line title of what this session was about>

## Where it started
<2-3 sentences: what Joshua asked for, key framing or constraints that emerged>

## Decisions locked + what shipped
- <decision or change> — <why, and where it lives (absolute path if a file)>
- ...

## Key files for next session
- `<absolute path>` — <why the next agent should read this first>
- Plan file: `<path>` (if a plan drove the session)
- Memory files touched: `<paths>` (if any)

## Running state
- Background processes: <shell IDs + what they are + how to kill> — or "none"
- Dev servers / ports: <url + port> — or "none"
- Open worktrees / branches: <paths> — or "none"

## Verified state as of <HH:MM>
- <claim about live/system state> — <how it was checked, and when>
- <claim> — NOT re-verified this session; last confirmed <when>
- ...

## Verification — how to confirm things still work
- `<command>` — <expected outcome>
- ...

## Deferred + open questions
- Deferred: <item> — <why pushed to later>
- OPEN (must be resolved or re-asked before new work): <question needing Joshua's input> — <context>

## Pick up here
<1-2 sentences: the single most likely next action for a fresh agent>
```

## Hard rules

1. **Chat output only.** Never write the handoff to a file. Never update memory from this skill. (That is `wrapup`'s job, not this one.)
2. **Never invent state.** If a section has nothing to report, write "none" — do not omit the section. Structure stability is the whole point.
3. **Absolute paths always.** The next agent may have a different working directory, or be Codex.
4. **If a plan file drove the session, name it first** in "Key files" so the next agent reads it before anything else.
5. **No emojis, no hype, no "great job" summaries.** Terse and concrete — paths, commands, shell IDs, decisions. Tone of a seasoned engineer handing off at end-of-shift.
6. **Background process IDs are critical.** If you started any `run_in_background` shells, their IDs must appear in "Running state" with the kill command — the next agent cannot find them otherwise.
7. **Stamp verified state, and separate it from assumed state.** Run `date "+%H:%M"` and use the real time. Every claim about live systems (deploy status, DB contents, what was sent, what is published) is either something you actually checked this session — say how — or explicitly marked NOT re-verified. A handoff that presents stale belief as current state makes the next session re-derive everything, which is the exact cost this skill exists to remove.
8. **Open questions are obligations, not notes.** Anything under OPEN must be resolved by the next session or re-asked to Joshua before it starts new work. Requirements have been silently re-typed across sessions, and a promised artifact once never arrived because nobody re-asked. If a question was open in the handoff you are picking up from, it is the first thing you raise.

## Anti-patterns — do not do these

- Summarizing the last 3 turns and calling it a handoff.
- Listing files by relative path.
- Skipping "Running state" because "nothing is running" — write "none" instead.
- Writing the summary to any file. This is chat-only by design.
- Adding a "what went well / what went poorly" retrospective. This isn't a retro.
- Recommending next steps beyond the single "Pick up here" line. The next agent decides; you just hand off.
